# Proposed OEIS comments: A252274 to A253750

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A252274

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252274>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [7931](../papers/07501-08000/07931-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 21*a(n-3) -62*a(n-6) +47*a(n-9) -5*a(n-12) for n>15

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 91 605 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 15. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A252305

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252305>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2439](../papers/02001-02500/02439-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252528>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [10204](../papers/10001-10500/10204-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-3) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(719 + 462*x + 626*x^2 - 2086*x^3 - 172*x^4) / (1 - 4*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Dec 04 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 447 707 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A252870

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252870>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4059](../papers/04001-04500/04059-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/24)*n^4 + (5/12)*n^3 - (13/24)*n^2 - (11/12)*n + 1.
> Empirical: G.f.: -x^2*(1+3*x-4*x^2+x^3) / (x-1)^5. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 21 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 15 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A253376

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A253376>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4381](../papers/04001-04500/04381-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 79 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 450 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 79, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A253478

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A253478>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4303](../papers/04001-04500/04303-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 88 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 114 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 88, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8974](../papers/08501-09000/08974-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) -33*a(n-2) +65*a(n-3) -75*a(n-4) +51*a(n-5) -19*a(n-6) +3*a(n-7) for n>15

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 42 669 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 15. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [586](../papers/00501-01000/00586-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
